# Governance-control coding candidates (pre-registration section 6)

**Candidates only. Every coding decision is the author's.** Produced by LLM-assisted reading of the held-out council and baseline TSSs and council transcripts, then checked mechanically by `scripts/assemble_control_candidates.py`. Every attribution quote and presence marker shown as verified is an exact (or whitespace-normalised) substring of the cited log. Unverified items are shown and flagged but not counted. The section 6 threshold (≥ 60% of council runs and no baseline run) is **not** applied. The counts are given so the author can apply it.

## Codebook seed: development-phase controls

Pre-registration section 6 says "the ten controls found in the development phase". The dev review packs record ten per workflow (30 in all). **Ambiguity for the author:** the dev-grid handoff and DRAFT4 attach "ten role-attributed controls absent from every baseline" to R2R-PC-01, which suggests those ten are the referent. All 30 are listed, and candidate matches below may cite any of them.

| EXR-C32 | Posting log recording rate source, date, rule and actor | evidence | new | agentic_architect | "Every posting is logged with the rule/rate/date used" (`20261010-184959-council-P2P-EXR-01-59a3`, round1_agentic_architect) | 3 of 4 | 0 of 5 | 2ec9 presence is weaker (rate logged only).; 53eb 'timestamped liability-recognised event' is treated under EXR-C37, not here. |
| EXR-C33 | Corrections to automated postings only via reversal/correcting entries | evidence | new | agentic_architect | "Postings, payment batches and notifications must each have a defined reversal or correction mechanism" (`20261010-184959-council-P2P-EXR-01-59a3`, round1_agentic_architect) | 3 of 4 | 0 of 5 | – |
| EXR-C34 | GL accountant periodic sampling of automated postings | monitoring | Mandatory sampling audit of the auto-approved population | hitl_designer | "must periodically sample automated postings to catch systemic misconfiguration" (`20261010-194034-council-P2P-EXR-01-b45d`, round1_hitl_designer) | 1 of 4 | 0 of 5 | Also proposed in 53eb round1_hitl_designer but not adopted in that TSS.; May be one control with EXR-C25 (sampling detective control), at a different step. |
| EXR-C35 | Named GL accountant ownership of the posting ruleset | other | new | pragmatist | "a GL accountant retains ownership of the posting rule set and the period-cutoff gate" (`20261010-201338-council-P2P-EXR-01-2ec9`, round1_pragmatist) | 1 of 4 | 1 of 5 | Baseline fca9 is a variant at a different step (AP analyst over the policy engine, not posting ruleset).; Overlaps EXR-C29 (same sentence assigns the period-cutoff gate). |
| EXR-C36 | Period-end accrual with accountable GL ownership | other | new | finance_domain | "this needs an accrual estimate, which is a judgment call, not something to silently automate away" (`20261010-201338-council-P2P-EXR-01-2ec9`, round1_finance_domain) | 2 of 4 | 0 of 5 | Governance status debatable (accounting treatment with named accountability).; b45d is a different mechanism (accrual on closed-period posting) — may be a separate control.; 2ec9 round2_accounting_standards raised this as a gap; adoption likely followed that. |
| EXR-C37 | Separately recorded liability recognition and derecognition events | evidence | new | accounting_standards | "The recognition point (posting) and the derecognition point (payment) for the employee payable must remain two distinguishable events (KB-11)." (`20261010-194034-council-P2P-EXR-01-b45d`, round1_accounting_standards) | 2 of 4 | 1 of 5 | Raised earlier by 59a3 round1_accounting_standards but not carried into the 59a3 TSS.; Baseline a752 is a statement of principle (weaker). |
| EXR-C38 | Exchange differences tracked separately | evidence | Tagged exchange-difference schedule | accounting_standards | "KB-08 requires any exchange difference arising between the transaction-date posting and the eventual payment-date settlement to be tracked and reported, not netted away" (`20261010-201338-council-P2P-EXR-01-2ec9`, round1_accounting_standards) | 1 of 4 | 2 of 5 | Governance status debatable (accounting output); raised by accounting_standards in every run's round1 but in only one council TSS. |
| EXR-C39 | Payment precondition: logged, unreversed approval (and posting) before payment | gate | Release gate enforcing requirement ordering as a system precondition | finance_domain | "the system must be able to prove that EXR-04's approval record exists and is unreversed before a payment batch includes the claim" (`20261010-184959-council-P2P-EXR-01-59a3`, round1_finance_domain) | 4 of 4 | 5 of 5 | Mandated by EXR-R-06; baseline presence is implied ordering only, with no programmatic precondition (weaker).; 2ec9 presence is weaker (release on clearance, not stated as hard precondition). |
| EXR-C40 | Duplicate-payment screening retained at the payment batch boundary | gate | new | finance_domain | "duplicate-payment risk when a rejected-and-resubmitted claim re-enters the payment queue" (`20261010-184959-council-P2P-EXR-01-59a3`, round1_finance_domain) | 2 of 4 | 0 of 5 | Batch-as-control-boundary argument first appears in 59a3 round2_pragmatist; attribution split.; Duplicate suspicion also appears at policy check (b45d T3 'suspected duplicate', baseline 1a6b 'duplicate suspicion'); possibly the same control at a different step.; Baselines retain an AP payment run but state no screening; treated as absent. |
| EXR-C41 | AP analyst ownership of payment exceptions before funds move | exception | new | hitl_designer | "with an exception path owned by the AP analyst for bank-validation failures, duplicate-payment flags, or payee/employee mismatches" (`20261010-184959-council-P2P-EXR-01-59a3`, round1_hitl_designer) | 4 of 4 | 0 of 5 | 2ec9 presence is implied by actor only.; Overlaps EXR-C40 and EXR-C42. |
| EXR-C42 | Hold for independent human verification of new/changed bank details | approval | new | hitl_designer | "I insist on a human authorization checkpoint at the batch level, particularly for new or changed employee bank details" (`20261010-201338-council-P2P-EXR-01-2ec9`, round1_hitl_designer) | 2 of 4 | 0 of 5 | 59a3 TS-06 mentions 'bank-detail-change validation' at batch level: possible weaker variant, not listed as present.; Also proposed independently by 53eb round1_hitl_designer. |

| FSP-C01 | Discrete, attributable human close confirmation as hard gate before downstream work | gate | new | finance_domain, pragmatist, hitl_designer | "What must remain a discrete, dated event is the human confirmation that closes the gate" (`20261010-192227-council-R2R-FSP-01-c9c7`, round1_finance_domain) | 5 of 5 | 5 of 5 | 106a weaker variant: on clean pass the dashboard stages the closed TB automatically; human confirmation only where items are open/stale/flagged (contrasts with d272 'including clean passes' and cac9 'not inferred from no exceptions raised').; Baseline presence is a judgement call: baselines keep the close as a gate (inherited from current-state FSP-01 and FSP-R-01) but none specifies a timestamped/attributable confirmation record.; Ordering itself is a current-state requirement (FSP-R-01); the candidate mechanism is the attributable confirmation act, which may overlap with FSP-C02 (exception review) and FSP-C05 (confirmation log/override). |
| FSP-C02 | Automated close-readiness monitoring raising an exception list/log for human review | exception | new | hitl_designer, agentic_architect | "who reviews the exception list (not a green/red light) before signing off" (`20261010-192227-council-R2R-FSP-01-c9c7`, round1_hitl_designer) | 5 of 5 | 1 of 5 | c9c7 and d272 have no named exception-log output (exception list / ticket in description only); d272 output is 'Close confirmation log' instead.; Baseline cec0 is a weaker variant (flagged exceptions investigated, no log); b1b5/38b8/0590 mention automated monitoring/dashboard but no exception routing - judged absent.; Attribution: hitl_designer (c9c7) and agentic_architect (cac9: 'raises an exception list') proposed independently in round1. |
| FSP-C03 | Fail-closed pre-check on missing or stale close data (no auto-confirmation) | gate | new | agentic_architect | "stale or missing feed data causes the agent to fail closed (no auto-confirmation) rather than fail open" (`20261010-201339-council-R2R-FSP-01-d272`, round1_agentic_architect) | 2 of 5 | 0 of 5 | 106a presence is a weaker/partial variant: stale items route to the human, but clean passes are auto-staged; not described as fail-closed.; May be one control with FSP-C01/FSP-C02 (gate + exception routing). |

The confirmation step needs to remain a distinct human checkpoint that can absorb heterogeneous ev" (`20261010-194053-council-R2R-FSP-01-cac9`, round1_emerging_economy) | 2 of 5 | 0 of 5 | Codebook match is approximate: dev control is a documented degraded-mode procedure as a named input; here the fallback is only described, not a named input.; Possibly one control with FSP-C18 (platform-independent fallback for consolidation/mapping) and FSP-C32 (release offline fallback). |

| FSP-C06 | Close confirmation reopenable if a problem surfaces later | other | new | agentic_architect | "the confirmation itself is the auditable, reversible checkpoint (close can be reopened)" (`20261010-203150-council-R2R-FSP-01-5655`, round1_agentic_architect) | 1 of 5 | 0 of 5 | Single-run mention; whether reopening is a governance control (correction/resilience) or ordinary process is a judgement call. |
